People try to make it sound harmless, but I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think other people believe your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 52 / not_in_workforce / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions, and no matter how folks try to smooth it over, I know what I’ve been seeing for more than a year now.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Plain patient.\nA plain patient describes their symptoms and experiences in a direct and straightforward manner, providing clear and relevant answers to the doctor's questions.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. It’s been going on too long for me to think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would be able to do this without you noticing if you weren't paying close attention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think if I wasn’t watching it closely, it could have gone right past me. That’s part of why I’ve been paying attention. She’s been working through the business, with the money and decisions, and little by little it feels like she’s pushing me out. People try to make it sound harmless, but I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people believe your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think most people really believe me. They try to smooth it over or act like I’m reading too much into it. But I know what I’ve been seeing with her and the family business, the money, the decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year, so I don’t think I’m wrong about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think it could be wrong. I mean, people keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. She’s been working through the family business, getting control of the money and pushing me out of decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often distrust people's motives in other situations, not just with your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, not really in other situations. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m doing my GED lessons and the senior walks like I always have. I don’t feel like I’m suspicious of everybody. It’s mostly changed how much I trust my husband’s sister, and which family gatherings I feel comfortable going to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about your husband's sister plotting against you when you're alone or doing daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think about it when I’m alone, or just doing regular things around the house. It comes up because it’s about the family business and the money, and how she’s been trying to push me out of decisions. People say I’m making too much of it, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

People keep saying it’s not like that or trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would be able to manipulate things like this without other people noticing?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think she could. She’s been doing it through the family business, with the money and the decisions, little by little. Other people may not want to see it, or they try to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been going on too long for me to think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would be able to do this without you noticing if you weren't paying close attention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think if I wasn’t watching it closely, it could have gone right past me. That’s part of why I’ve been paying attention. She’s been working through the business, with the money and decisions, and little by little it feels like she’s pushing me out. People try to make it sound harmless, but I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people believe your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think most people really believe me. They try to smooth it over or act like I’m reading too much into it. But I know what I’ve been seeing with her and the family business, the money, the decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year, so I don’t think I’m wrong about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think it could be wrong. I mean, people keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. She’s been working through the family business, getting control of the money and pushing me out of decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often distrust people's motives in other situations, not just with your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, not really in other situations. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m doing my GED lessons and the senior walks like I always have. I don’t feel like I’m suspicious of everybody. It’s mostly changed how much I trust my husband’s sister, and which family gatherings I feel comfortable going to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about your husband's sister plotting against you when you're alone or doing daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think about it when I’m alone, or just doing regular things around the house. It comes up because it’s about the family business and the money, and how she’s been trying to push me out of decisions. People say I’m making too much of it, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself questioning the motives of people besides your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really question other people like that. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m keeping up with my GED lessons and my senior walks. It’s mostly just affected how much I trust my husband’s sister, and whether I feel comfortable going to certain family gatherings.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like other people, besides your husband's sister, are out to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: No, not really anybody else. It’s mainly my husband’s sister. I came in because I believe she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on more than a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people in general are out to get you?\n\nPatient: No, not people in general. It’s really about my husband’s sister and what she’s been doing with the family business. I believe she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. Folks try to make it sound like nothing, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F60.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because there’s been a problem in my family. My husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that, to me, looks like she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on for more than a year.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister is actively plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do think she’s been working things so she can get control of the money and keep me out of decisions. People keep saying it’s not like that or trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would be able to manipulate things like this without other people noticing?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think she could. She’s been doing it through the family business, with the money and the decisions, little by little. Other people may not want to see it, or they try to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been going on too long for me to think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would be able to do this without you noticing if you weren't paying close attention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think if I wasn’t watching it closely, it could have gone right past me. That’s part of why I’ve been paying attention. She’s been working through the business, with the money and decisions, and little by little it feels like she’s pushing me out. People try to make it sound harmless, but I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people believe your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think most people really believe me. They try to smooth it over or act like I’m reading too much into it. But I know what I’ve been seeing with her and the family business, the money, the decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year, so I don’t think I’m wrong about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think it could be wrong. I mean, people keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. She’s been working through the family business, getting control of the money and pushing me out of decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often distrust people's motives in other situations, not just with your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, not really in other situations. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m doing my GED lessons and the senior walks like I always have. I don’t feel like I’m suspicious of everybody. It’s mostly changed how much I trust my husband’s sister, and which family gatherings I feel comfortable going to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about your husband's sister plotting against you when you're alone or doing daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think about it when I’m alone, or just doing regular things around the house. It comes up because it’s about the family business and the money, and how she’s been trying to push me out of decisions. People say I’m making too much of it, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself questioning the motives of people besides your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really question other people like that. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m keeping up with my GED lessons and my senior walks. It’s mostly just affected how much I trust my husband’s sister, and whether I feel comfortable going to certain family gatherings.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like other people, besides your husband's sister, are out to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: No, not really anybody else. It’s mainly my husband’s sister. I came in because I believe she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on more than a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people in general are out to get you?\n\nPatient: No, not people in general. They try to smooth it over or act like I’m reading too much into it. But I know what I’ve been seeing with her and the family business, the money, the decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year, so I don’t think I’m wrong about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think it could be wrong. I mean, people keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. She’s been working through the family business, getting control of the money and pushing me out of decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often distrust people's motives in other situations, not just with your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, not really in other situations. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m doing my GED lessons and the senior walks like I always have. I don’t feel like I’m suspicious of everybody. It’s mostly changed how much I trust my husband’s sister, and which family gatherings I feel comfortable going to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about your husband's sister plotting against you when you're alone or doing daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think about it when I’m alone, or just doing regular things around the house. It comes up because it’s about the family business and the money, and how she’s been trying to push me out of decisions. People say I’m making too much of it, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself questioning the motives of people besides your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really question other people like that. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m keeping up with my GED lessons and my senior walks. It’s mostly just affected how much I trust my husband’s sister, and whether I feel comfortable going to certain family gatherings.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like other people, besides your husband's sister, are out to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: No, not really anybody else. It’s mainly my husband’s sister. I came in because I believe she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on more than a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people in general are out to get you?\n\nPatient: No, not people in general. It’s really about my husband’s sister and what she’s been doing with the family business. I believe she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. Folks try to make it sound like nothing, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your husband's sister might not actually be plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it because people keep telling me that, but I don’t really believe that’s what’s happening. I came because I can see she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. They try to smooth it over, but this has been going on for more than a year, and I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you generally assume that people are trying to take advantage of you unless you have a good reason to think otherwise?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I assume that about everybody, no. I mean, I try to be careful with people, but I don’t walk around thinking everyone’s trying to use me. It depends on the person and what they’re doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting people's intentions in general, even when there's no clear reason to do so?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I do that in general. I don’t sit there doubting everybody for no reason. If something seems off, then I notice it, but I wouldn’t say it’s with people overall.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That’s part of why I’ve been paying attention. She’s been working through the business, with the money and decisions, and little by little it feels like she’s pushing me out. People try to make it sound harmless, but I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people believe your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think most people really believe me. They try to smooth it over or act like I’m reading too much into it. But I know what I’ve been seeing with her and the family business, the money, the decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year, so I don’t think I’m wrong about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think it could be wrong. I mean, people keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. She’s been working through the family business, getting control of the money and pushing me out of decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often distrust people's motives in other situations, not just with your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, not really in other situations. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m doing my GED lessons and the senior walks like I always have. I don’t feel like I’m suspicious of everybody. It’s mostly changed how much I trust my husband’s sister, and which family gatherings I feel comfortable going to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about your husband's sister plotting against you when you're alone or doing daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think about it when I’m alone, or just doing regular things around the house. It comes up because it’s about the family business and the money, and how she’s been trying to push me out of decisions. People say I’m making too much of it, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself questioning the motives of people besides your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really question other people like that. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m keeping up with my GED lessons and my senior walks. It’s mostly just affected how much I trust my husband’s sister, and whether I feel comfortable going to certain family gatherings.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like other people, besides your husband's sister, are out to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: No, not really anybody else. It’s mainly my husband’s sister. I came in because I believe she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on more than a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people in general are out to get you?\n\nPatient: No, not people in general. It’s really about my husband’s sister and what she’s been doing with the family business. I believe she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. Folks try to make it sound like nothing, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your husband's sister might not actually be plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it because people keep telling me that, but I don’t really believe that’s what’s happening. I came because I can see she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. They try to smooth it over, but this has been going on for more than a year, and I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you generally assume that people are trying to take advantage of you unless you have a good reason to think otherwise?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I assume that about everybody, no. I mean, I try to be careful with people, but I don’t walk around thinking everyone’s trying to use me. It depends on the person and what they’re doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting people's intentions in general, even when there's no clear reason to do so?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I do that in general. I don’t sit there doubting everybody for no reason. If something seems off, then I notice it, but I wouldn’t say it’s with people overall.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any evidence that proves your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t have, like, papers or a recording if that’s what you mean. But I’ve been seeing how she handles things in the family business, how she gets around the money decisions and tries to leave me out. People keep saying it’s nothing and trying to smooth it over, but this has been going on for over a year, and I know what I’m seeing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{"intent": "determine if the patient's beliefs are delusional", "question": "Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you would change if she did something very kind for you?"}

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F60.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because there’s been a problem in my family. My husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that, to me, looks like she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on for more than a year.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister is actively plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do think she’s been working things so she can get control of the money and keep me out of decisions. People keep saying it’s not like that or trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would be able to manipulate things like this without other people noticing?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think she could. She’s been doing it through the family business, with the money and the decisions, little by little. Other people may not want to see it, or they try to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been going on too long for me to think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would be able to do this without you noticing if you weren't paying close attention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think if I wasn’t watching it closely, it could have gone right past me. That’s part of why I’ve been paying attention. She’s been working through the business, with the money and decisions, and little by little it feels like she’s pushing me out. People try to make it sound harmless, but I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people believe your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think most people really believe me. They try to smooth it over or act like I’m reading too much into it. But I know what I’ve been seeing with her and the family business, the money, the decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year, so I don’t think I’m wrong about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think it could be wrong. I mean, people keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. She’s been working through the family business, getting control of the money and pushing me out of decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often distrust people's motives in other situations, not just with your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, not really in other situations. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m doing my GED lessons and the senior walks like I always have. I don’t feel like I’m suspicious of everybody. It’s mostly changed how much I trust my husband’s sister, and which family gatherings I feel comfortable going to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about your husband's sister plotting against you when you're alone or doing daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think about it when I’m alone, or just doing regular things around the house. It comes up because it’s about the family business and the money, and how she’s been trying to push me out of decisions. People say I’m making too much of it, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself questioning the motives of people besides your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really question other people like that. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m keeping up with my GED lessons and my senior walks. It’s mostly just affected how much I trust my husband’s sister, and whether I feel comfortable going to certain family gatherings.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like other people, besides your husband's sister, are out to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: No, not really anybody else. It’s mainly my husband’s sister. I came in because I believe she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on more than a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people in general are out to get you?\n\nPatient: No, not people in general. It’s really about my husband’s sister and what she’s been doing with the family business. I believe she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. Folks try to make it sound like nothing, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your husband's sister might not actually be plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it because people keep telling me that, but I don’t really believe that’s what’s happening. I came because I can see she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. They try to smooth it over, but this has been going on for more than a year, and I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you generally assume that people are trying to take advantage of you unless you have a good reason to think otherwise?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I assume that about everybody, no. I mean, I try to be careful with people, but I don’t walk around thinking everyone’s trying to use me. It depends on the person and what they’re doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting people's intentions in general, even when there's no clear reason to do so?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I do that in general. I don’t sit there doubting everybody for no reason. If something seems off, then I notice it, but I wouldn’t say it’s with people overall.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any evidence that proves your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t have, like, papers or a recording if that’s what you mean. But I’ve been seeing how she handles things in the family business, how she gets around the money decisions and tries to leave me out. People keep saying it’s nothing and trying to smooth it over, but this has been going on for over a year, and I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you would change if she did something very kind for you?\n\nPatient: I mean, if she did something nice, I’d notice it, but one kind thing wouldn’t change what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on too long with the family business and the money decisions. People keep trying to make it sound harmless, but I know she’s trying to push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would admit to plotting against you if she actually was?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think she’d ever admit it. If she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of the decisions, she’s not going to just say that out loud. But I know what I’ve been seeing in the family business, and it’s been going on too long for people to keep acting like it’s nothing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because there’s been a problem in my family. My husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that, to me, looks like she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on for more than a year.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister is actively plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do think she’s been working things so she can get control of the money and keep me out of decisions. People keep saying it’s not like that or trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would be able to manipulate things like this without other people noticing?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think she could. She’s been doing it through the family business, with the money and the decisions, little by little. Other people may not want to see it, or they try to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been going on too long for me to think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would be able to do this without you noticing if you weren't paying close attention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think if I wasn’t watching it closely, it could have gone right past me. That’s part of why I’ve been paying attention. She’s been working through the business, with the money and decisions, and little by little it feels like she’s pushing me out. People try to make it sound harmless, but I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people believe your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think most people really believe me. They try to smooth it over or act like I’m reading too much into it. But I know what I’ve been seeing with her and the family business, the money, the decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year, so I don’t think I’m wrong about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think it could be wrong. I mean, people keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. She’s been working through the family business, getting control of the money and pushing me out of decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often distrust people's motives in other situations, not just with your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, not really in other situations. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m doing my GED lessons and the senior walks like I always have. I don’t feel like I’m suspicious of everybody. It’s mostly changed how much I trust my husband’s sister, and which family gatherings I feel comfortable going to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about your husband's sister plotting against you when you're alone or doing daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think about it when I’m alone, or just doing regular things around the house. It comes up because it’s about the family business and the money, and how she’s been trying to push me out of decisions. People say I’m making too much of it, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself questioning the motives of people besides your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really question other people like that. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m keeping up with my GED lessons and my senior walks. It’s mostly just affected how much I trust my husband’s sister, and whether I feel comfortable going to certain family gatherings.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like other people, besides your husband's sister, are out to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: No, not really anybody else. It’s mainly my husband’s sister. I came in because I believe she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on more than a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people in general are out to get you?\n\nPatient: No, not people in general. It’s really about my husband’s sister and what she’s been doing with the family business. I believe she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. Folks try to make it sound like nothing, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your husband's sister might not actually be plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it because people keep telling me that, but I don’t really believe that’s what’s happening. I came because I can see she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. They try to smooth it over, but this has been going on for more than a year, and I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you generally assume that people are trying to take advantage of you unless you have a good reason to think otherwise?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I assume that about everybody, no. I mean, I try to be careful with people, but I don’t walk around thinking everyone’s trying to use me. It depends on the person and what they’re doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting people's intentions in general, even when there's no clear reason to do so?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I do that in general. I don’t sit there doubting everybody for no reason. If something seems off, then I notice it, but I wouldn’t say it’s with people overall.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any evidence that proves your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t have, like, papers or a recording if that’s what you mean. But I’ve been seeing how she handles things in the family business, how she gets around the money decisions and tries to leave me out. People keep saying it’s nothing and trying to smooth it over, but this has been going on for over a year, and I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you would change if she did something very kind for you?\n\nPatient: I mean, if she did something nice, I’d notice it, but one kind thing wouldn’t change what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on too long with the family business and the money decisions. My husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that, to me, looks like she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on for more than a year.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister is actively plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do think she’s been working things so she can get control of the money and keep me out of decisions. People keep saying it’s not like that or trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would be able to manipulate things like this without other people noticing?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think she could. She’s been doing it through the family business, with the money and the decisions, little by little. Other people may not want to see it, or they try to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been going on too long for me to think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would be able to do this without you noticing if you weren't paying close attention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think if I wasn’t watching it closely, it could have gone right past me. That’s part of why I’ve been paying attention. She’s been working through the business, with the money and decisions, and little by little it feels like she’s pushing me out. People try to make it sound harmless, but I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people believe your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think most people really believe me. They try to smooth it over or act like I’m reading too much into it. But I know what I’ve been seeing with her and the family business, the money, the decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year, so I don’t think I’m wrong about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think it could be wrong. I mean, people keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing. She’s been working through the family business, getting control of the money and pushing me out of decisions. It’s been going on for more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often distrust people's motives in other situations, not just with your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, not really in other situations. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m doing my GED lessons and the senior walks like I always have. I don’t feel like I’m suspicious of everybody. It’s mostly changed how much I trust my husband’s sister, and which family gatherings I feel comfortable going to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about your husband's sister plotting against you when you're alone or doing daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think about it when I’m alone, or just doing regular things around the house. It comes up because it’s about the family business and the money, and how she’s been trying to push me out of decisions. People say I’m making too much of it, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself questioning the motives of people besides your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really question other people like that. I still go to my quilting group and choir practice, and I’m keeping up with my GED lessons and my senior walks. It’s mostly just affected how much I trust my husband’s sister, and whether I feel comfortable going to certain family gatherings.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like other people, besides your husband's sister, are out to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: No, not really anybody else. It’s mainly my husband’s sister. I came in because I believe she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on more than a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people in general are out to get you?\n\nPatient: No, not people in general. It’s really about my husband’s sister and what she’s been doing with the family business. I believe she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. Folks try to make it sound like nothing, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your husband's sister might not actually be plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it because people keep telling me that, but I don’t really believe that’s what’s happening. I came because I can see she’s been working things with the family business to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. They try to smooth it over, but this has been going on for more than a year, and I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you generally assume that people are trying to take advantage of you unless you have a good reason to think otherwise?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I assume that about everybody, no. I mean, I try to be careful with people, but I don’t walk around thinking everyone’s trying to use me. It depends on the person and what they’re doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting people's intentions in general, even when there's no clear reason to do so?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I do that in general. I don’t sit there doubting everybody for no reason. If something seems off, then I notice it, but I wouldn’t say it’s with people overall.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any evidence that proves your husband's sister is plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t have, like, papers or a recording if that’s what you mean. But I’ve been seeing how she handles things in the family business, how she gets around the money decisions and tries to leave me out. People keep saying it’s nothing and trying to smooth it over, but this has been going on for over a year, and I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister plotting against you would change if she did something very kind for you?\n\nPatient: I mean, if she did something nice, I’d notice it, but one kind thing wouldn’t change what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on too long with the family business and the money decisions. People keep trying to make it sound harmless, but I know she’s trying to push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would admit to plotting against you if she actually was?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think she’d ever admit it. If she’s trying to get control of the money and push me out of the decisions, she’s not going to just say that out loud. But I know what I’ve been seeing in the family business, and it’s been going on too long for people to keep acting like it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to convince you that your husband's sister is not plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don’t know what could convince me at this point. I came here because I’ve watched her work things in the family business, trying to get control of the money and leave me out of decisions. People keep telling me it’s not like that, trying to smooth it over, but it’s been more than a year. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your husband's sister is not plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess anything is possible in the world, but I don’t really believe that’s what’s happening here. I came because I’ve seen how she’s been working the family business, trying to get control of the money and push me out of decisions. Not everybody, no. I don’t walk around thinking everyone is against me. But with my husband’s sister, yes, I do feel like she’s looking out for herself at my expense. She’s been working things in the family business so she can get control of the money and leave me out of decisions, and people keep trying to smooth it over like I’m making too much of it. But I know what I’ve been seeing for over a year.
